\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}.

Moreover, Chapoton and Livernet
[
7
]
have shown that
({\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\},\curvearrowright)
is the free pre-Lie algebra generated by
\{\scalebox{0.6}{
\hbox to4.17pt{\vbox to4.17pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.08551pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.61 0 C 2.61 1.44 1.44 2.61 0 2.61 C -1.44 2.61 -2.61 1.44 -2.61 0 C -2.61 -1.44 -1.44 -2.61 0 -2.61 C 1.44 -2.61 2.61 -1.44 2.61 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.4}{0.0}{0.0}{0.4}{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}\}
.
By Theorem
2.9
, we deduce that
(T{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\},\cdot,\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}},\rhd)
is a post-Hopf algebra. Since
({\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\},\curvearrowright)
is a pre-Lie algebra, the post-Hopf algebra structure reduces to the symmetric algebra
S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\}
. Thus, we deduce that
(S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\},\cdot,\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}},\rhd)
is a pre-Hopf algebra. Furthermore, it is the universal enveloping algebra of the free pre-Lie algebra
({\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\},\curvearrowright)
, and its subadjacent Hopf algebra

(S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\},*_{\rhd},\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}},S_{\rhd})
is dual to the Connes-Kreimer Hopf algebra of rooted trees.

3.
Relative Rota-Baxter operators on Hopf algebras

In this section, first we recall relative Rota-Baxter operators on Lie algebras and groups, and Rota-Baxter operators on cocommutative Hopf algebras. Then we introduce a more general notion of relative Rota-Baxter operators of weight 1 on cocommutative Hopf algebras with respect to module bialgebras. We establish the relation between the category of relative Rota-Baxter operators of weight 1 on cocommutative Hopf algebras and the category of post-Hopf algebras.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{h}\rightarrow{\rm Der}(\mathfrak{k})
be an action of a Lie
algebra
(\mathfrak{h},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{h}})
on a Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{k},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{k}})
. A linear map
T:\mathfrak{k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}
is called a
relative Rota-Baxter operator (of weight
1
)
on
\mathfrak{h}
with respect
to
(\mathfrak{k};\phi)
if

(14)

[T(u),T(v)]_{\mathfrak{h}}=T\Big(\phi(T(u))v-\phi(T(v))u+[u,v]_{\mathfrak{k}}\Big),\quad\forall u,v\in\mathfrak{k}.

Let
\Phi:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow{\rm Aut}(\mathcal{K})
be an action of a group
\mathcal{H}
on a group
\mathcal{K}
. A map
T:\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
is called a
relative Rota-Baxter operator (of weight
1
)
if

(15)

T(h)\cdot_{\mathcal{H}}T(k)=T(h\cdot_{\mathcal{K}}\Phi(T(h))k),\quad\forall h,k\in\mathcal{K}.

Given any Hopf algebra
(H,\Delta,\varepsilon,S)
, define the adjoint action of
H
on itself by

{\rm ad}_{x}y=x_{1}yS(x_{2}).

A
Rota-Baxter operator
(of weight 1)
on a cocommutative Hopf algebra
H
was defined by Goncharov in
[
21
]
, which is a coalgebra homomorphism
B
satisfying

(16)

B(x)B(y)=B\left(x_{1}{\rm ad}_{B(x_{2})}y\right)=B\left(x_{1}B(x_{2})yS(B(x_{3}))\right),\quad\forall\,x,y\in H.

In the sequel, all the (relative) Rota-Baxter operators under consideration are of weight 1, so we will not emphasize it anymore.

Now we generalize the above adjoint action to arbitrary actions and introduce the notion of relative Rota-Baxter operators on Hopf algebras.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
H
and
K
be two Hopf algebras such that
K
is a left
H
-module bialgebra via an action
\rightharpoonup
. A coalgebra homomorphism
T:K\to H
is called a
relative Rota-Baxter operator
with respect to the left
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
if the following
equality holds:

(17)

\displaystyle T(a)T(b)=T\big(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b)\big),\quad\forall a,b\in K.

A
homomorphism
between two relative Rota-Baxter operators

T:K\to H
and
T^{\prime}:K^{\prime}\to H^{\prime}
is a pair of Hopf algebra homomorphisms
f:H\to H^{\prime}
and
g:K\to K^{\prime}
such that

(18)

\displaystyle fT=T^{\prime}g,\quad g(x\rightharpoonup a)=f(x)\rightharpoonup g(a),\quad\forall\,x\in H,\,a\in K.

It is obvious that relative Rota-Baxter operators on Hopf algebras and homomorphisms between them form a category, which is denoted by
\mathsf{rRB}
. We denote by
\mathsf{cocrRB}
the subcategory of
\mathsf{rRB}
consisting of relative Rota-Baxter operators with respect to cocommutative left module bialgebras and homomorphisms between them.

A cocommutative post-Hopf algebra naturally gives rise to a relative Rota-Baxter operator.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
(H,\rhd)
be a cocommutative post-Hopf algebra and
H_{\rhd}
the subadjacent Hopf algebra. Then
the identity map
{\rm id}_{H}:H\to H_{\rhd}
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator with respect to the left
H_{\rhd}
-module bialgebra
(H,\rhd)
.

Moreover, if
g:H\to H^{\prime}
is a post-Hopf algebra homomorphism from
(H,\rhd)
to
(H^{\prime},\rhd^{\prime})
, then
(g,g)
is a homomorphism from the relative Rota-Baxter operator
{\rm id}_{H}:H\to H_{\rhd}
to
{\rm id}_{H^{\prime}}:H^{\prime}\to H^{\prime}_{\rhd^{\prime}}
. Consequently, we obtain a functor
\Upsilon:\mathsf{cocPH}\to\mathsf{cocrRB}
from the category of cocommutative post-Hopf algebras to the category of relative Rota-Baxter operators with respect to cocommutative left module bialgebras.

Proof.

For any
x,y,z\in H
, we have

{\rm id}_{H}(x)*_{\rhd}{\rm id}_{H}(y)=x*_{\rhd}y=x_{1}\cdot(x_{2}\rhd y)={\rm id}_{H}(x_{1}\cdot({\rm id}_{H}(x_{2})\rhd y)),

so
{\rm id}_{H}:H\to H_{\rhd}
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator with respect to the left
H_{\rhd}
-module bialgebra
(H,\rhd)
.

Let
g:H\to H^{\prime}
be a post-Hopf algebra homomorphism from
(H,\rhd)
to
(H^{\prime},\rhd^{\prime})
. Then
(g,g)
obviously satisfy Eq. (
18
). Since
g
is a coalgebra homomorphism and

\displaystyle g(x*_{\rhd}y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g(x_{1}\cdot(x_{2}\rhd y))=g(x_{1})\cdot^{\prime}(g(x_{2})\rhd^{\prime}g(y))=g(x)*_{\rhd^{\prime}}g(y),

we deduce that
g
is a homomorphism from the Hopf algebra
H_{\rhd}
to
H^{\prime}_{\rhd^{\prime}}
. Therefore,
(g,g)
is a homomorphism from the relative Rota-Baxter operator
{\rm id}_{H}:H\to H_{\rhd}
to
{\rm id}_{H^{\prime}}:H^{\prime}\to H^{\prime}_{\rhd^{\prime}}
. It is straightforward to check that this is indeed a functor.
∎

It is well-known that a relative Rota-Baxter operator
T:\mathfrak{k}\to\mathfrak{h}
on a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}
with respect to an action
(\mathfrak{k};\phi)
endows
\mathfrak{k}
with the following post-Lie algebra structure
\rhd_{T}
,

(19)

u\rhd_{T}v=\phi(T(u))v,\quad\forall u,v\in\mathfrak{k}.

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator with respect to a left
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
. Then there exists a post-Hopf algebra structure
\rhd_{T}:K\otimes K\to K
on
K
given by

(20)

\displaystyle a\rhd_{T}b=T(a)\rightharpoonup b.

Let
T:K\to H
and
T^{\prime}:K^{\prime}\to H^{\prime}
be two relative Rota-Baxter operators and
(f,g)
a homomorphism between them. Then
g
is a homomorphism from the post-Hopf algebra
(K,\rhd_{T})
to
(K^{\prime},\rhd_{T^{\prime}})
. Consequently, we obtain a functor
\Xi:\mathsf{rRB}\to\mathsf{PH}
from the category of relative Rota-Baxter operators on Hopf algebras to the category of post-Hopf algebras.

Moreover, the functor
\Xi|_{\mathsf{cocrRB}}
is right adjoint to the functor
\Upsilon
given in Proposition
3.2
.

Proof.

Since
T
is a coalgebra homomorphism and
\rightharpoonup
is the left module bialgebra action, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{K}(a\rhd_{T}b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta_{K}(T(a)\rightharpoonup b)=(T(a_{1})\rightharpoonup b_{1})\otimes(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b_{2})=(a_{1}\rhd_{T}b_{1})\otimes(a_{2}\rhd_{T}b_{2}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K}(a\rhd_{T}b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K}(T(a)\rightharpoonup b)=\varepsilon_{H}(T(a))\varepsilon_{K}(b)=\varepsilon_{K}(a)\varepsilon_{K}(b),

which implies that
\rhd_{T}
is a coalgebra homomorphism. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle a\rhd_{T}(bc)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a)\rightharpoonup(bc)=(T(a_{1})\rightharpoonup b)(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup c)=(a_{1}\rhd_{T}b)(a_{2}\rhd_{T}c).

Then by (
17
), we obtain

\displaystyle(a_{1}(a_{2}\rhd_{T}b))\rhd_{T}c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b))\rightharpoonup c=(T(a)T(b))\rightharpoonup c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a)\rightharpoonup(T(b)\rightharpoonup c)=a\rhd_{T}(b\rhd_{T}c).

Define linear map
S_{T}:K\to K
by

(21)

\displaystyle S_{T}(a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a_{1}))\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{2}).

Then for all
a\in K
, we have

(22)

\displaystyle T(S_{T}(a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{H}(T(a_{1}))T(S_{T}(a_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a_{1}))T(a_{2})T(S_{T}(a_{3}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:rrb-Hopf}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a_{1}))T(a_{2}(T(a_{3})\rightharpoonup S_{T}(a_{4})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a_{1}))T(a_{2}(T(a_{3})\rightharpoonup(S_{H}(T(a_{4}))\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{5}))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a_{1}))T(a_{2}(T(a_{3})S_{H}(T(a_{4}))\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{5})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a_{1}))T(a_{2}S_{K}(a_{3}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a_{1}))T(\varepsilon_{K}(a_{2})1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a)).

For all
a\in K
, define
\beta_{\rhd_{T},a}\in{\rm End}(K)
by
\beta_{\rhd_{T},a}\coloneqq\alpha_{\rhd_{T},S_{T}(a)}.
That is,

\beta_{\rhd_{T},a}b=\alpha_{\rhd_{T},S_{T}(a)}b=S_{T}(a)\rhd_{T}b.

Then we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rhd_{T},a_{1}}\beta_{\rhd_{T},a_{2}}b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1})\rightharpoonup(T(S_{T}(a_{2}))\rightharpoonup b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1})T(S_{T}(a_{2}))\rightharpoonup b

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:rrb-Hopf}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle T(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup S_{T}(a_{3})))\rightharpoonup b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup(S_{H}(T(a_{3}))\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{4}))))\rightharpoonup b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1}(T(a_{2})S_{H}(T(a_{3}))\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{4})))\rightharpoonup b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1}S_{K}(a_{2}))\rightharpoonup b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(\varepsilon_{K}(a)1)\rightharpoonup b=\varepsilon_{K}(a)b,

\displaystyle\beta_{\rhd_{T},a_{1}}\alpha_{\rhd_{T},a_{2}}b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(S_{T}(a_{1}))\rightharpoonup(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(S_{T}(a_{1}))T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:rrb-anti}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle S_{H}(T(a_{1}))T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{H}(T(a))b=\varepsilon_{K}(a)b.

Therefore,
\alpha_{\rhd_{T}}
is convolution invertible. Hence,
(K,\rhd_{T})
is a post-Hopf algebra.

Let
(f,g)
be a homomorphism from the relative Rota-Baxter operator
T
to
T^{\prime}
. Then we have

\displaystyle g(a\rhd_{T}b)=g(T(a)\rightharpoonup b)=f(T(a))\rightharpoonup g(b)=T^{\prime}(g(a))\rightharpoonup g(b)=g(a)\rhd_{T^{\prime}}g(b),

which implies that
g
is a homomorphism from the post-Hopf algebra
(K,\rhd_{T})
to
(K^{\prime},\rhd_{T^{\prime}})
. It is straightforward to see that this is indeed a functor.

Next we prove that
\Xi|_{\mathsf{cocrRB}}:\mathsf{cocrRB}\to\mathsf{cocPH}
is right adjoint to
\Upsilon:\mathsf{cocPH}\to\mathsf{cocrRB}
.
Namely,

{\rm Hom}_{\mathsf{cocrRB}}({\rm id}:H^{\prime}\to H^{\prime}_{\rhd^{\prime}},T:K\to H)\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\mathsf{cocPH}}((H^{\prime},\rhd^{\prime}),(K,\rhd_{T})),

where
T:K\to H
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator on a Hopf algebra
H
with respect to a cocommutative module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
and
(H^{\prime},\rhd^{\prime})
is a cocommutative post-Hopf algebra.

Let
g:(H^{\prime},\rhd^{\prime})\to(K,\rhd_{T})
be a post-Hopf algebra homomorphism. Let
f=Tg
, which is obviously a coalgebra homomorphism. For all
x,y\in H^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle f(x*_{\rhd^{\prime}}y)

\displaystyle=T(g(x_{1}(x_{2}\rhd^{\prime}y)))=T(g(x_{1})(g(x_{2})\rhd_{T}g(y)))

\displaystyle=T(g(x_{1})(T(g(x_{2}))\rightharpoonup g(y)))=T(g(x))T(g(y))=f(x)f(y),

which implies that
f=Tg:H^{\prime}_{\rhd^{\prime}}\to H
is a Hopf algebra homomorphism.

Moreover, it is straightforward to obtain

g(x\rhd^{\prime}y)=g(x)\rhd_{T}g(y)=T(g(x))\rightharpoonup g(y)=f(x)\rightharpoonup g(y).

Hence,
(f,g)
is a homomorphism between the relative Rota-Baxter operators
{\rm id}:H^{\prime}\to H^{\prime}_{\rhd^{\prime}}
and
T:K\to H
.

Conversely, if
(f,g)
is a homomorphism between the relative Rota-Baxter operators
{\rm id}:H^{\prime}\to H^{\prime}_{\rhd^{\prime}}
and
T:K\to H
, we have
f=Tg
and
g:(H^{\prime},\rhd^{\prime})\to(K,\rhd_{T})
is a post-Hopf algebra homomorphism.
∎

By Theorem
3.3
and Theorem
2.4
, we immediately get the following result.

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator
with respect to a cocommutative
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
. Then
(K,*_{T},1,\Delta,\varepsilon,S_{T})
is a Hopf algebra, which is called the
descendent Hopf algebra
and denoted by
K_{T}
, where the antipode
S_{T}
is given by (
21
) and the multiplication
*_{T}
is given by

(23)

\displaystyle a*_{T}b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b).

Moreover,
T:K_{T}\to H
is a Hopf algebra homomorphism.

4.
Matched pairs of Hopf algebras and solutions of the Yang-Baxter equation

In this section, we show that a relative Rota-Baxter operator on cocommutative Hopf algebras naturally gives rise to a matched pair of Hopf algebras. As applications, we construct solutions of the Yang-Baxter equation using post-Hopf algebras and relative Rota-Baxter operators on cocommutative Hopf algebras.

First we recall the smash product and matched pairs of Hopf algebras.
Let
H
and
K
be two Hopf algebras such that
K
is a cocommutative
H
-module bialgebra via an action
\rightharpoonup
.
There is the following
smash product
on
K\otimes H
,

(a\#x)(a^{\prime}\#x^{\prime})=a(x_{1}\rightharpoonup a^{\prime})\#x_{2}x^{\prime}

for any
x,x^{\prime}\in H,\,a,a^{\prime}\in K
, where
a\otimes x\in K\otimes H
is rewritten as
a\#x
to emphasize this smash product. We denote such a smash product algebra by
K\rtimes H
.
In particular, if
H
is also cocommutative, then
K\rtimes H
becomes a cocommutative Hopf algebra with the usual tensor product comultiplication and the antipode defined by
S(a\#x)=(S_{H}(x_{1})\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a))\#S_{H}(x_{2})
.

Definition 4.1
.

A
matched pair of Hopf algebras
is a 4-tuple
(H,K,\rightharpoonup,\leftharpoonup)
, where
H
and
K

are Hopf algebras,
\rightharpoonup:H\otimes K\to K
and
\leftharpoonup:H\otimes K\to H
are linear maps such that
K
is a left
H
-module coalgebra and
H
is a right
K
-module coalgebra and the following compatibility
conditions hold:

(24)

\displaystyle x\rightharpoonup(ab)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(x_{1}\rightharpoonup a_{1})((x_{2}\leftharpoonup a_{2})\rightharpoonup b)

(25)

\displaystyle x\rightharpoonup 1_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{H}(x)1_{K}

(26)

\displaystyle(xy)\leftharpoonup a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(x\leftharpoonup(y_{1}\rightharpoonup a_{1}))(y_{2}\leftharpoonup a_{2})

(27)

\displaystyle 1_{H}\leftharpoonup a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K}(a)1_{H}

(28)

\displaystyle(x_{1}\leftharpoonup a_{1})\otimes(x_{2}\rightharpoonup a_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(x_{2}\leftharpoonup a_{2})\otimes(x_{1}\rightharpoonup a_{1})

for all
x,y\in H
and
a,b\in K
.

Let
(H,K,\rightharpoonup,\leftharpoonup)
be a matched pair of Hopf algebras. The
double crossproduct

K\bowtie H
of
K
and
H
is the
{\mathbf{k}}
-vector space
K\otimes H
with the unit

1_{K}\otimes 1_{H}
, such that its product, coproduct, counit and antipode are given by

(29)

\displaystyle(a\otimes x)(b\otimes y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a(x_{1}\rightharpoonup b_{1})\otimes(x_{2}\leftharpoonup b_{2})y,

(30)

\displaystyle\Delta(a\otimes x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}\otimes x_{1}\otimes a_{2}\otimes x_{2},

(31)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(a\otimes x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K}(a)\varepsilon_{H}(x),

(32)

\displaystyle S(a\otimes x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(S_{H}(x_{2})\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{2}))\otimes(S_{H}(x_{1})\leftharpoonup S_{K}(a_{1})),

for all
a,b\in K
and
x,y\in H
. See
[
30
]
for further details of the double crossproducts.

By
[
30
, Proposition 21.6]
, we have

Theorem 4.2
.

With above notations,
(H,K,\rightharpoonup,\leftharpoonup)
is a matched pair of Hopf algebras if and only if there exist a Hopf algebra
A
and injective Hopf algebra homomorphisms
i_{K}:K\to A
,
i_{H}:H\to A
such that
the map

\xi:K\otimes H\to A,\,a\otimes x\mapsto i_{K}(a)i_{H}(x)

is a linear isomorphism.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator
with respect to a cocommutative
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
.
Define a linear map
\leftharpoonup:H\otimes K\to H
by

(33)

\displaystyle x\leftharpoonup a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{H}\big(T(x_{1}\rightharpoonup a_{1})\big)x_{2}T(a_{2}).

Theorem 4.3
.

With the above notations, if
H
is also cocommutative, then it is a right
K_{T}
-module coalgebra via the action
\leftharpoonup
given in Eq. (
33
). Moreover, the
4
-tuple
(H,K_{T},\rightharpoonup,\leftharpoonup)
is a matched pair of cocommutative Hopf algebras.

Proof.

We define a linear map
\Phi_{T}:K\otimes H\to K\otimes H
as following:

(34)

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}(a\otimes x)=a_{1}\otimes T(a_{2})x,~\forall x\in H,\,a\in K.

Since
T
is a coalgebra homomorphism, the linear map
\Phi_{T}
is invertible. Moreover, we have

(35)

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}(a\otimes x)=a_{1}\otimes S_{H}(T(a_{2}))x,~\forall x\in H,\,a\in K.

Transfer the smash product Hopf algebra structure
K\rtimes H
to
K\otimes H
via the linear isomorphism
\Phi_{T}:K\otimes H\to K\rtimes H
, we obtain a Hopf algebra
(K\otimes H,\cdot_{T},1_{T},\Delta_{T},\varepsilon_{T},\mathfrak{S}_{T})
. Denote elements in
K\otimes H
by
a\bowtie x,~b\bowtie y
for
x,y\in H,\,a,b\in K
, by the cocommutativity of
K
, we have

\displaystyle(a\bowtie x)\cdot_{T}(b\bowtie y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}\big(\Phi_{T}(a\bowtie x)\Phi_{T}(b\bowtie y)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}^{-1}\big(a_{1}(T(a_{2})x_{1}\rightharpoonup b_{1})\#T(a_{3})x_{2}T(b_{2})y\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}(T(a_{2})x_{1}\rightharpoonup b_{1})\bowtie S_{H}\big(T(a_{3}(T(a_{4})x_{2}\rightharpoonup b_{2}))\big)T(a_{5})x_{3}T(b_{3})y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup(x_{1}\rightharpoonup b_{1}))\bowtie S_{H}\big(T(a_{3}(T(a_{4})\rightharpoonup(x_{2}\rightharpoonup b_{2})))\big)T(a_{5})x_{3}T(b_{3})y

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:rrb-Hopf}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle a_{1}*_{T}(x_{1}\rightharpoonup b_{1})\bowtie S_{H}\big(T(a_{2})T(x_{2}\rightharpoonup b_{2})\big)T(a_{3})x_{3}T(b_{3})y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}*_{T}(x_{1}\rightharpoonup b_{1})\bowtie S_{H}\big(T(x_{2}\rightharpoonup b_{2})\big)S_{H}(T(a_{2}))T(a_{3})x_{3}T(b_{3})y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a*_{T}(x_{1}\rightharpoonup b_{1})\bowtie S_{H}\big(T(x_{2}\rightharpoonup b_{2})\big)x_{3}T(b_{3})y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a\ast_{T}(x_{1}\rightharpoonup b_{1})\bowtie(x_{2}\leftharpoonup b_{2})y,

\displaystyle\Delta_{T}(a\bowtie x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Phi^{-1}_{T}\otimes\Phi^{-1}_{T})(\Delta\Phi_{T}(a\bowtie x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Phi^{-1}_{T}\otimes\Phi^{-1}_{T})\Delta(a_{1}\#T(a_{2})x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Phi^{-1}_{T}\otimes\Phi^{-1}_{T})\big((a_{1}\#T(a_{3})x_{1})\otimes(a_{2}\#T(a_{4})x_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\bowtie S_{H}(T(a_{2}))T(a_{5})x_{1})\otimes(a_{3}\bowtie S_{H}(T(a_{4}))T(a_{6})x_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\bowtie S_{H}(T(a_{2}))T(a_{3})x_{1})\otimes(a_{4}\bowtie S_{H}(T(a_{5}))T(a_{6})x_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\varepsilon_{K}(a_{2})a_{1}\bowtie x_{1})\otimes(\varepsilon_{K}(a_{4})a_{3}\bowtie x_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\bowtie x_{1})\otimes(a_{2}\bowtie x_{2}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{T}(a\bowtie x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}(S_{\rtimes}\Phi_{T}(a\bowtie x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}(S_{\rtimes}(a_{1}\#T(a_{2})x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}((S_{H}(T(a_{1})x_{1})\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{2}))\#S_{H}(T(a_{3})x_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}((S_{H}(x_{1})\rightharpoonup(S_{H}(T(a_{1}))\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{2})))\#S_{H}(T(a_{3})x_{2}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rrb-des2}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}((S_{H}(x_{1})\rightharpoonup S_{T}(a_{1}))\#S_{H}(x_{2})S_{H}(T(a_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(S_{H}(x_{1})\rightharpoonup S_{T}(a_{1}))\bowtie S_{H}(T(S_{H}(x_{2})\rightharpoonup S_{T}(a_{2})))S_{H}(x_{3})S_{H}(T(a_{3}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(S_{H}(x_{1})\rightharpoonup S_{T}(a_{1}))\bowtie S_{H}(T(S_{H}(x_{2})\rightharpoonup S_{T}(a_{2})))S_{H}(x_{3})T(S_{T}(a_{3}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:mp-raction}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle(S_{H}(x_{1})\rightharpoonup S_{T}(a_{1}))\bowtie(S_{H}(x_{2})\leftharpoonup S_{T}(a_{2})).

Moreover, it is obvious that
1_{T}=1\bowtie 1
and

\varepsilon_{T}(a\bowtie x)=\varepsilon_{K}(a_{1})\varepsilon_{H}(T(a_{2})x)=\varepsilon_{K}(a)\varepsilon_{H}(x).

Define linear maps
\mathfrak{i}_{K_{T}}:K_{T}\to K\otimes H
and
\mathfrak{i}_{H}:H\to K\otimes H
by

\mathfrak{i}_{K_{T}}(a)=a\bowtie 1,\quad\mathfrak{i}_{H}(x)=1\bowtie x.

Then it is obvious that
\mathfrak{i}_{K_{T}}
and
\mathfrak{i}_{H}
are injective Hopf algebra homomorphisms, and

\displaystyle(a\bowtie 1)\cdot_{T}(1\bowtie x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a\ast_{T}(1\rightharpoonup 1)\bowtie(1\leftharpoonup 1)x=a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup 1)\bowtie x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}\varepsilon_{H}(T(a_{2}))\bowtie x=a\bowtie x.

Therefore, we obtain that
(K\otimes H,\cdot_{T},1_{T},\Delta_{T},\varepsilon_{T},\mathfrak{S}_{T})
is a Hopf algebra that can be factorized into Hopf algebras
K_{T}
and
H
. Thus, we deduce that
H
is a right
K_{T}
-module coalgebra via the action
\leftharpoonup
and
K_{T}
is a left
H
-module coalgebra via the action
\rightharpoonup
and the 4-tuples
(H,K_{T},\rightharpoonup,\leftharpoonup)
is a matched pair of Hopf algebras by Theorem
4.2
. Moreover, the Hopf algebra
(K\otimes H,\cdot_{T},1_{T},\Delta_{T},\varepsilon_{T},\mathfrak{S}_{T})
is exactly the double crossproduct
K_{T}\bowtie H.

∎

Conversely, let
H
and
K
be two cocommutative Hopf algebras such that
K
is an
H
-module bialgebra via an action
\rightharpoonup
. Let
T:K\to H
be a coalgebra homomorphism, and
(K\otimes H,\cdot_{T},1_{T},\Delta_{T},\varepsilon_{T},\mathfrak{S}_{T})
the Hopf algebra obtained from the smash product
K\rtimes H
via the linear isomorphism
\Phi_{T}
given in (
34
).

Proposition 4.4
.

If
K\otimes 1
is a subalgebra of the Hopf algebra
(K\otimes H,\cdot_{T},1_{T},\Delta_{T},\varepsilon_{T},\mathfrak{S}_{T})
,
then
T
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator
with respect to the
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
.

Proof.

Since
K\otimes 1
is a subalgebra of
(K\otimes H,\cdot_{T},1_{T},\Delta_{T},\varepsilon_{T},\mathfrak{S}_{T})
, for any
a,b\in K
we have

\displaystyle(a\bowtie 1)\cdot_{T}(b\bowtie 1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}\big(\Phi_{T}(a\bowtie 1)\Phi_{T}(b\bowtie 1)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{T}\big(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b_{1})\#T(a_{3})T(b_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b_{1})\bowtie S_{H}\big(T(a_{3}(T(a_{4})\rightharpoonup b_{2}))\big)T(a_{5})T(b_{3})\in K\otimes 1.

Applying
m_{H}(T\otimes{\rm id})
and
T\otimes\varepsilon_{H}
to it respectively, we obtain that

\displaystyle T(a)T(b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b_{1}))S_{H}\big(T(a_{3}(T(a_{4})\rightharpoonup b_{2}))\big)T(a_{5})T(b_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b_{1}))\varepsilon_{H}(S_{H}\big(T(a_{3}(T(a_{4})\rightharpoonup b_{2}))\big)T(a_{5})T(b_{3}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b_{1})).

Namely, (
17
) holds, and
T
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator.
∎

Let
(H,\rhd)
be a post-Hopf algebra and
H_{\rhd}\coloneqq(H,*_{\rhd},1,\Delta,\varepsilon,S_{\rhd})
the subadjacent Hopf algebra given in Theorem
2.4
. By Proposition
3.2
, the identity map
{\rm id}:H\to H_{\rhd}
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator. By Theorem
4.3
, we have

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
(H,\rhd)
be a cocommutative post-Hopf algebra. Then the
4
-tuple
(H_{\rhd},H_{\rhd},\rhd,\lhd)
is a matched pair of cocommutative Hopf algebras, where
\lhd
is given by

(36)

\displaystyle a\lhd b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{\rhd}\big(a_{1}\rhd b_{1}\big)\ast_{\rhd}a_{2}\ast_{\rhd}b_{2}.

Moreover, we have the compatibility condition

(37)

\displaystyle a\ast_{\rhd}b=(a_{1}\rhd b_{1})\ast_{\rhd}(a_{2}\lhd b_{2}).

Proof.

We only need to check the stated compatibility condition, which follows from

\displaystyle(a_{1}\rhd b_{1})\ast_{\rhd}(a_{2}\lhd b_{2})

\displaystyle=(a_{1}\rhd b_{1})\ast_{\rhd}\big(S_{\rhd}\big(a_{2}\rhd b_{2}\big)\ast_{\rhd}a_{3}\ast_{\rhd}b_{4}\big)

\displaystyle=\big((a_{1}\rhd b_{1})\ast_{\rhd}S_{\rhd}\big(a_{2}\rhd b_{2}\big)\big)\ast_{\rhd}a_{3}\ast_{\rhd}b_{4}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(a_{1}\rhd b_{1})a_{2}\ast_{\rhd}b_{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(a_{1})\varepsilon(b_{1})a_{2}\ast_{\rhd}b_{2}

\displaystyle=a\ast_{\rhd}b.\qed

At the end of this section, we show that post-Hopf algebras and relative Rota-Baxter operators on cocommutative Hopf algebras give rise to solutions of the Yang-Baxter equation.

Definition 4.6
.

A solution of the Yang-Baxter equation on a vector space
V

is an invertible linear endomorphism
R:V\otimes V\to V\otimes V
such that

(R\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes R)(R\otimes{\rm id})=({\rm id}\otimes R)(R\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes R).

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
(H,\rhd)
be a cocommutative post-Hopf algebra. Then
R:H\otimes H\to H\otimes H
defined by

R(x\otimes y)=(x_{1}\rhd y_{1})\otimes(x_{2}\lhd y_{2}),

where
\lhd
is defined by (
33
), is a coalgebra isomorphism and a solution of the Yang-Baxter equation on the vector space
H
.

Proof.

Denote by
H_{\rhd}^{l}
and
H_{\rhd}^{r}
two copies of the Hopf algebra
H_{\rhd}
. By Corollary
4.5
,
(H_{\rhd}^{l},H_{\rhd}^{r},\rhd,\lhd)
is a matched pair of cocommutative Hopf algebras. Thus,
A=H_{\rhd}^{l}\bowtie H_{\rhd}^{r}
is a Hopf algebra that factorized into Hopf algebras
H_{\rhd}^{l}
and
H_{\rhd}^{r}
. By Theorem
4.2
, there is a coalgebra isomorphism

\xi:H_{\rhd}^{l}\otimes H_{\rhd}^{r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathfrak{i}_{H_{\rhd}^{l}}\otimes\mathfrak{i}_{H_{\rhd}^{r}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}A\otimes A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m_{H_{\rhd}^{l}\bowtie H_{\rhd}^{r}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}A=H_{\rhd}^{l}\bowtie H_{\rhd}^{r}.

We consider the coalgebra homomorphism

\Psi=\xi^{-1}\circ m_{H_{\rhd}^{l}\bowtie H_{\rhd}^{r}}\circ(\mathfrak{i}_{H_{\rhd}^{r}}\otimes\mathfrak{i}_{H_{\rhd}^{l}}):H_{\rhd}^{r}\otimes H_{\rhd}^{l}\to H_{\rhd}^{l}\otimes H_{\rhd}^{r}.

Since
H_{\rhd}^{l}\bowtie H_{\rhd}^{r}
is a cocommutative Hopf algebra, we deduce that
\Psi
is a coalgebra isomorphism. Moreover,
\Psi
satisfies the following equations:

\displaystyle\Psi\circ(m_{H_{\rhd}}\otimes{\rm Id})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm Id}\otimes m_{H_{\rhd}})\circ(\Psi\otimes{\rm Id})\circ({\rm Id}\otimes\Psi),

\displaystyle\Psi\circ({\rm Id}\otimes m_{H_{\rhd}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m_{H_{\rhd}}\otimes{\rm Id})\circ({\rm Id}\otimes\Psi)\circ(\Psi\otimes{\rm Id}),

\displaystyle\Psi(1\otimes x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x\otimes 1,

\displaystyle\Psi(x\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1\otimes x.

For all
x,y\in H
, we have

\displaystyle\Psi(x\otimes y)=(x_{1}\rhd y_{1})\otimes(x_{2}\lhd y_{2})=R(x\otimes y).

By (
37
), we have
m_{H_{\rhd}}=m_{H_{\rhd}}\circ\Psi
. Thus, we deduce that
R=\Psi
is a braiding operator on the cocommutative Hopf algebra
H_{\rhd}\coloneqq(H,*_{\rhd},1,\Delta,\varepsilon,S_{\rhd})
. By
[
23
, Theorem 4.11]
, we obtain that
R
is a solution of the Yang-Baxter equation on the vector space
H
.
∎

Example 4.8
.

Consider the post-Hopf algebra
(T{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{OT}}\},\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}},\rhd)
given in Example
2.10
. Then
R:T{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{OT}}\}\otimes T{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{OT}}\}\to T{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{OT}}\}\otimes T{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{OT}}\}
defined by

R(\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{Y})=\Big(\mathcal{X}_{1}\rhd\mathcal{Y}_{1}\Big)\otimes\Big(\mathcal{X}_{2}\lhd\mathcal{Y}_{2}\Big),\quad\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\in T{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{OT}}\}

is a coalgebra isomorphism and a solution of the Yang-Baxter equation on the vector space
T{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{OT}}\}
. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle R(\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{Y})=\Big(\mathcal{X}_{1}\rhd\mathcal{Y}_{1}\Big)\otimes B^{-}\Big(S_{\rhd}\big(\mathcal{X}_{2}\rhd\mathcal{Y}_{2}\big)\rhd\big(\mathcal{X}_{3}\rhd B^{+}(\mathcal{Y}_{3})\big)\Big),

where
{\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}}}^{(2)}\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{3}
and
{\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}}}^{(2)}\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{Y}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Y}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{Y}_{3}
.

Example 4.9
.

Consider the pre-Hopf algebra
(S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\},\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}},\rhd)
given in Example
2.18
. Then
R:S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\}\otimes S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\}\to S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\}\otimes S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\}
defined by

R(\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{Y})=\Big(\mathcal{X}_{1}\rhd\mathcal{Y}_{1}\Big)\otimes\Big(\mathcal{X}_{2}\lhd\mathcal{Y}_{2}\Big),\quad\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\in S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\}

is a coalgebra isomorphism and a solution of the Yang-Baxter equation on the vector space
S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\}
. More precisely, for forests
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\in S{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{T}}\}
, we have

\displaystyle R(\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{Y})=\Big(\mathcal{X}_{1}\rhd\mathcal{Y}_{1}\Big)\otimes B^{-}\Big(S_{\rhd}\big(\mathcal{X}_{2}\rhd\mathcal{Y}_{2}\big)\rhd\big(\mathcal{X}_{3}\rhd B^{+}(\mathcal{Y}_{3})\big)\Big),

where
{\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}}}^{(2)}\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{3}
and
{\Delta^{\mathsf{cosh}}}^{(2)}\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{Y}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Y}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{Y}_{3}
.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator on
H
with respect to a commutative
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
. By Theorem
3.3
,
(K,\rhd_{T})
is a commutative post-Hopf algebra. By Corollary
3.4
, there is a descendent Hopf algebra
K_{T}=(K,*_{T},\Delta,\varepsilon,S_{T})
, such that
K
is a
K_{T}
-module bialgebra via the action
\rhd_{T}
defined in (
20
). By Corollary
4.5
, we have

Corollary 4.10
.

The
4
-tuples
(K_{T},K_{T},\rhd_{T},\lhd_{T})
is a matched pair of cocommutative Hopf algebras, here
\rhd_{T}
is given by (
20
) and
\lhd_{T}
is given by

(38)

\displaystyle a\lhd_{T}b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{T}\big(a_{1}\rhd_{T}b_{1}\big)\ast_{T}a_{2}\ast_{T}b_{2}.

Moreover, we have the compatibility condition

(39)

\displaystyle a\ast_{T}b=(a_{1}\rhd_{T}b_{1})\ast_{T}(a_{2}\lhd_{T}b_{2}).

By Theorem
4.7
, we have

Corollary 4.11
.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator with respect to a commutative
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
. Then
R:K\otimes K\to K\otimes K
defined by

R(a\otimes b)=\Big(a_{1}\rhd_{T}b_{1}\Big)\otimes\Big(a_{2}\lhd_{T}b_{2}\Big)

is a coalgebra isomorphism and a solution of the Yang-Baxter equation on the vector space
K
, where
\rhd_{T}
and
\lhd_{T}
are defined by (
20
) and (
38
) respectively.

5.
Equivalent characterizations of relative Rota-Baxter operators

In this section, we give some alternative characterizations of relative Rota-Baxter operators using relative Rota-Baxter operators on the Lie algebra of primitive elements, graphs and module bialgebra structures.

5.1.
Restrictions and extensions of relative Rota-Baxter operators

Let
K
be a cocommutative
H
-module bialgebra via an action
\rightharpoonup
. It is obvious that via the restrictions of the action
\rightharpoonup
, we obtain actions of
G(H)
on
G(K)
and of
P(H)
on
P(K)
, for which we use the same notations.
As expected, a relative Rota-Baxter operator with respect to a cocommutative
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
will naturally induces a relative Rota-Baxter operator on the group
G(H)
and on the Lie algebra
P(H)
respectively.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator with respect to a cocommutative
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
.

(i)

T|_{G(K)}
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator on the group
G(H)
with respect to the action
(G(K),\rightharpoonup)
;

(ii)

T|_{P(K)}
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator on the Lie algebra
P(H)
with respect to the action
(P(K),\rightharpoonup)
.

Proof.

Since
T
is a coalgebra homomorphism, it follows that
T|_{G(K)}
is a map from
G(K)
to
G(H)
, and
T|_{P(K)}
is a map from
P(K)
to
P(H)
.

For any
a,b\in G(K)
, we have

T(a)T(b)=T\big(a(T(a)\rightharpoonup b)\big),

which implies that
T|_{G(K)}
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator on the group
G(H)
with respect to the action
(G(K),\rightharpoonup)
.

For any
a,b\in P(K)
, we have

T(a)T(b)=T(ab)+T\big(T(a)\rightharpoonup b\big),

and thus

[T(a),T(b)]=T\big(T(a)\rightharpoonup b\big)-T\big(T(b)\rightharpoonup a\big)+T([a,b]).

Hence,
T|_{P(K)}
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator on the Lie algebra
P(H)
with respect to the action
(P(K),\rightharpoonup)
.
∎

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{h}\to{\rm Der}(\mathfrak{k})
be an action of a Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{h},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{h}})
on
(\mathcal{\mathfrak{k}},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{k}})
. Then
\phi
can be extended to a module bialgebra action
\bar{\phi}:U(\mathfrak{h})\to{\rm End}(T_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathfrak{k}))
by

\bar{\phi}(x)(1)=0,\,\bar{\phi}(x)(y_{1}\cdots y_{r})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}y_{1}\cdots y_{i-1}\phi(x)(y_{i})y_{i+1}\cdots y_{r},

where
T_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathfrak{k})
is the tensor
{\mathbf{k}}
-algebra of
\mathfrak{k}
,
x\in\mathfrak{h}
and
y_{1},\dots,y_{r}\in\mathfrak{k},\,r\geq 1
. As
\mathfrak{h}
acts on
\mathfrak{k}
by derivations, it induces a module bialgebra action
\bar{\phi}
of
U(\mathfrak{h})
on
U(\mathfrak{k})
.

The following extension theorem of relative Rota-Baxter operators from Lie algebras to their universal enveloping algebras generalizes the case of Rota-Baxter operators given in
[
21
, Theorem 2]
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Any relative Rota-Baxter operator
T:\mathfrak{k}\to\mathfrak{h}
on a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}
with respect to an action
(\mathfrak{k};\phi)
can be extended to a unique relative Rota-Baxter operator
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
with respect to the extended
U(\mathfrak{h})
-module bialgebra
(U(\mathfrak{k}),\bar{\phi})
by

\bar{T}(y_{1}\cdots y_{n})=\big(T(y_{1})\bar{T}-\bar{T}\bar{\phi}(T(y_{1}))\big)\cdots\big(T(y_{n})\bar{T}-\bar{T}\bar{\phi}(T(y_{n}))\big)(1),\quad\forall y_{1},\dots,y_{n}\in\mathfrak{k},\,n\geq 1,

where those
T(y_{k})
’s left to
\bar{T}
are interpreted as the left multiplication by them.

Furthermore, the post-Hopf algebra
(U(\mathfrak{k}),\rhd_{\bar{T}})
induced by the relative Rota-Baxter operator
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
as in Theorem
3.3
coincides with the extended post-Hopf algebra
(U(\mathfrak{k}),\bar{\rhd}_{T})
from
(\mathfrak{k},\rhd_{T})
given in Theorem
2.8
. Namely, we have the following diagram

(40)

\textstyle{(\mathfrak{k},\rhd_{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
extension
\textstyle{(U(\mathfrak{k}),\rhd_{\bar{T}})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
extension
Rota-Baxter action
\textstyle{U(\mathfrak{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bar{T}}}{{\to}}U(\mathfrak{h})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
Rota-Baxter action

Proof.

Let
J_{\mathfrak{k}}=(yz-zy-[y,z]_{\mathfrak{k}}\,|\,y,z\in\mathfrak{k})
be the ideal of
T_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathfrak{k})
such that
U(\mathfrak{k})\simeq T_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathfrak{k})/J_{\mathfrak{k}}
.
We recursively define a linear map
\bar{T}:T_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
by

(41)

\bar{T}(1)=1,\quad\bar{T}(yu)=T(y)\bar{T}(u)-\bar{T}\big(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u\big),\quad\forall y\in\mathfrak{k},\,u\in\mathfrak{k}^{\otimes n},\,n\geq 0.

Then it is straightforward to deduce that
\bar{T}(J_{\mathfrak{k}})=0
and we have the induced linear map
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
.

Next we prove that
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator. Namely,

\bar{T}(u)\bar{T}(v)=\bar{T}\big(u_{1}\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u_{2}))v\big)

for any
u\in U(\mathfrak{k})_{m},v\in U(\mathfrak{k})_{n}
. It can be done by induction on
m
. The case when
m=1
is due to the recursive definition (
41
) of
\bar{T}
. For
yu\in U(\mathfrak{k})_{m+1}
, since
\bar{\phi}
is a module bialgebra action, we have

\displaystyle\bar{T}(yu)\bar{T}(v)

\displaystyle=T(y)\bar{T}(u)\bar{T}(v)-\bar{T}\big(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u\big)\bar{T}(v)

\displaystyle=T(y)\bar{T}\big(u_{1}\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u_{2}))v\big)-\bar{T}\big(\big(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u_{1}\big)\big(\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u_{2}))v\big)\big)-\bar{T}\big(u_{1}\big(\bar{\phi}\big(\bar{T}(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u_{2})\big)v\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\bar{T}\big(yu_{1}\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u_{2}))v\big)+\bar{T}\big(\bar{\phi}(T(y))\big(u_{1}\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u_{2}))v\big)\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\bar{T}\big(\big(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u_{1}\big)\big(\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u_{2}))v\big)\big)-\bar{T}\big(u_{1}\big(\bar{\phi}\big(\bar{T}(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u_{2})\big)v\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\bar{T}\big(yu_{1}\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u_{2}))v\big)+\bar{T}\big(u_{1}\bar{\phi}\big(T(y)\bar{T}(u_{2})\big)v\big)-\bar{T}\big(u_{1}\big(\bar{\phi}\big(\bar{T}(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u_{2})\big)v\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\bar{T}\big(yu_{1}\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u_{2}))v\big)+\bar{T}\big(u_{1}\bar{\phi}\big(\bar{T}(yu_{2})\big)v\big)

\displaystyle=\bar{T}\big((yu)_{1}\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}((yu)_{2}))v\big),

which implies that
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator.
The above procedure also implies that the extension from
T:\mathfrak{k}\to\mathfrak{h}
to
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
is unique.

By (
19
), the induced post-Lie product
\rhd_{T}
on
\mathfrak{k}
is given by

y\rhd_{T}z=\phi(T(y))z,\quad\forall y,z\in\mathfrak{k}.

Then by Theorem
2.8
, the extended post-Hopf product
\bar{\rhd}_{T}

on
U(\mathfrak{k})
is recursively defined by

\displaystyle y\bar{\rhd}_{T}1=0,\quad y\bar{\rhd}_{T}zv=(y\rhd_{T}z)v+z(y\bar{\rhd}_{T}v),

\displaystyle 1\bar{\rhd}_{T}v=v,\quad yu\bar{\rhd}_{T}v=y\bar{\rhd}_{T}(u\bar{\rhd}_{T}v)-(y\bar{\rhd}_{T}u)\bar{\rhd}_{T}v,

for any
y,z\in\mathfrak{k},\,u,v\in U(\mathfrak{k})
.
On the other hand, by (
20
), we know that

u\rhd_{\bar{T}}v=\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u))v,\quad\forall u,v\in U(\mathfrak{k}).

In particular,
y\rhd_{\bar{T}}1=0
,
1\rhd_{\bar{T}}v=v
and

\displaystyle y\rhd_{\bar{T}}zv

\displaystyle=\bar{\phi}(T(y))(zv)=(\phi(T(y))z)v+z\bar{\phi}(T(y))v

\displaystyle=(y\rhd_{T}z)v+z(y\rhd_{\bar{T}}v),

\displaystyle yu\rhd_{\bar{T}}v

\displaystyle=\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(yu))v=\bar{\phi}\big(T(y)\bar{T}(u)-\bar{T}\big(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u\big)\big)v

\displaystyle=\bar{\phi}(T(y))\big(\bar{\phi}(\bar{T}(u))v\big)-\bar{\phi}\big(\bar{T}\big(\bar{\phi}(T(y))u\big)\big)v

\displaystyle=y\rhd_{\bar{T}}(u\rhd_{\bar{T}}v)-(y\rhd_{\bar{T}}u)\rhd_{\bar{T}}v.

Therefore, the two post-Hopf products on
U(\mathfrak{k})
coincide, and we get the desired diagram.
∎

5.2.
Graph characterization

Now we use graphs to characterize relative Rota-Baxter operators
on Hopf algebras.

Definition 5.3
.

Given any coalgebra homomorphism
f:K\to H
, we define the
graph
of
f
, which is denoted by
{\rm Gr}_{f}
, as the subspace
{\rm im}(({\rm id}\otimes f)\Delta_{K})
of
K\otimes H
, namely,

{\rm Gr}_{f}=\{a_{1}\otimes f(a_{2})\,|\,a\in K\}.

Theorem 5.4
.

A coalgebra homomorphism
T:K\to H
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator with respect to a cocommutative
H
-module bialgebra
(K,\rightharpoonup)
if and only if the graph
{\rm Gr}_{T}
is a Hopf subalgebra of the smash product Hopf algebra
K\rtimes H
and isomorphic to
K_{T}
.

Proof.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator. Then for all
a,b\in K
, we have

\displaystyle(a_{1}\#T(a_{2}))(b_{1}\#T(b_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b_{1})\#T(a_{3})T(b_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}\ast_{T}b_{1}\#T(a_{2})T(b_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}\ast_{T}b_{1}\#T(a_{2}\ast_{T}b_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a\ast_{T}b)_{1}\#T((a\ast_{T}b)_{2})\in{\rm Gr}_{T},

as the binary operation
\ast_{T}
on
K
defined in (
23
) is a coalgebra homomorphism by the cocommutativity of
K
, which implies that
{\rm Gr}_{T}
is a subalgebra of
K\rtimes H
with unit
1\#1=1\#T(1)
.

Also, as
T
is a coalgebra homomorphism and
K
is cocommutative,

\displaystyle\Delta(a_{1}\#T(a_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\#T(a_{3}))\otimes(a_{2}\#T(a_{4}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\#T(a_{2}))\otimes(a_{3}\#T(a_{4}))\in{\rm Gr}_{T}\otimes{\rm Gr}_{T},

\displaystyle S(a_{1}\#T(a_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(S_{H}(T(a_{1}))\rightharpoonup S_{K}(a_{2}))\#S_{H}(T(a_{3}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rrb-des2}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle S_{T}(a_{1})\#T(S_{T}(a_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{T}(a)_{1}\#T(S_{T}(a)_{2})\in{\rm Gr}_{T},

where the antipode formula above is due to the property of
S_{T}
shown in Proposition
3.4
. Hence,
{\rm Gr}_{T}
is a Hopf algebra inside
K\rtimes H
. Furthermore, define linear map

(42)

\Psi:K_{T}\to{\rm Gr}_{T},\,a\mapsto a_{1}\#T(a_{2}).

By the calculation above, we know that
\Psi
is a Hopf algebra isomorphism with its inverse
\Psi^{-1}={\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{H}
.

Conversely, assume that
{\rm Gr}_{T}
is a subalgebra of
K\rtimes H
. For any
a,b\in K
, there exists
w\in K
such that

a_{1}\ast_{T}b_{1}\#T(a_{2})T(b_{2})=(a_{1}\#T(a_{2}))(b_{1}\#T(b_{2}))=w_{1}\#T(w_{2}).

In particular, since
\ast_{T}
is bilinear, we have

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle=w_{1}\varepsilon_{H}(T(w_{2}))=a_{1}\ast_{T}b_{1}\varepsilon_{H}(T(a_{2})T(b_{2}))=a\ast_{T}b,

\displaystyle T(w)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{K}(w_{1})T(w_{2})=\varepsilon_{K}(a_{1}\ast_{T}b_{1})T(a_{2})T(b_{2})=T(a)T(b),

which indicate that
T
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator.
∎

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{h}\to{\rm Der}(\mathfrak{k})
be an action of a Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{h},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{h}})
on a Lie algebra
(\mathcal{\mathfrak{k}},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{k}})
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{k}\rtimes\mathfrak{h}
the semi-direct product Lie algebra of
\mathfrak{k}
and
\mathfrak{h}
with respect to the action
(\mathfrak{k},\phi)
. More precisely, the Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\rtimes}:\wedge^{2}(\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{h})\to\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{h}
is given by

\displaystyle~[(u,x),(v,y)]_{\rtimes}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle([u,v]_{\mathfrak{k}}+\phi(x)(v)-\phi(y)(u),[x,y]_{\mathfrak{h}}),\quad\forall x,y\in\mathfrak{h},~u,v\in\mathfrak{k}.

It is well known that
T:\mathfrak{k}\to\mathfrak{h}
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator if and only if the graph of
T
,
{\rm Gr}_{T}:=\{(u,T(u))\,|\,u\in\mathfrak{k}\}
is a subalgebra of
\mathfrak{k}\rtimes\mathfrak{h}
.

Now we consider the lifted relative Rota-Baxter operator
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
of the relative Rota-Baxter operator
T:\mathfrak{k}\to\mathfrak{h}
. It turns out that the Hopf algebra
{\rm Gr}_{\bar{T}}
can serve as the universal enveloping algebra of the Lie algebra
{\rm Gr}_{T}.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
be the lifted relative Rota-Baxter operator of the relative Rota-Baxter operator
T:\mathfrak{k}\to\mathfrak{h}
. Then
{\rm Gr}_{\bar{T}}\simeq U({\rm Gr}_{T}),
i.e. the universal enveloping algebra of the graph
{\rm Gr}_{T}
of the relative Rota-Baxter operator
T:\mathfrak{k}\to\mathfrak{h}
is isomorphic to the Hopf algebra
{\rm Gr}_{\bar{T}}
, which is the graph of the relative Rota-Baxter operator
\bar{T}:U(\mathfrak{k})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
.

Proof.

First it is straightforward to obtain the following Hopf algebra isomorphism

\bar{\varphi}:U(\mathfrak{k}\rtimes\mathfrak{h})\to U(\mathfrak{k})\rtimes U(\mathfrak{h}),\quad(u,x)\mapsto u\#1+1\#x,\quad\forall x\in\mathfrak{h},~u\in\mathfrak{k}.

By Theorem
5.4
,
{\rm Gr}_{\bar{T}}
is a Hopf subalgebra of
U(\mathfrak{k})\#U(\mathfrak{h})
isomorphic to
U(\mathfrak{k})_{\bar{T}}
. Let
\psi\coloneqq\bar{\varphi}|_{{\rm Gr}_{T}}
, then
\psi
is injective and

\psi(u,T(u))=u\#1+1\#T(u)=({\rm id}\otimes\bar{T})\Delta(u)\in{\rm Gr}_{\bar{T}},\quad\forall u\in\mathfrak{k}.

Also, note that
{\rm Im}\,\psi
generates
{\rm Gr}_{\bar{T}}
as an algebra. Hence,
\psi
induces a Hopf algebra isomorphism
\bar{\psi}:U({\rm Gr}_{T})\to{\rm Gr}_{\bar{T}}
by the Theorem of Heyneman and Radford
[
32
, Theorem 5.3.1]
, as
U({\rm Gr}_{T})_{1}={\mathbf{k}}\oplus{\rm Gr}_{T}
and
\bar{\psi}|_{U({\rm Gr}_{T})_{1}}
is also injective.
∎

5.3.
Module and module bialgebra characterization

Next we give another characterization of relative Rota-Baxter operators on Hopf algebras using new module structures and new module bialgebra structures. Let
H
and
K
be Hopf algebras such that
K
is a cocommutative
H
-module bialgebra via an action
\rightharpoonup
.

Theorem 5.6
.

A coalgebra homomorphism
T:K\to H
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator if and only if
K
endowed with the binary operation
*_{T}
in (
23
) is an algebra, denoted by
K_{T}=(K,*_{T})
, and
H
is a
K_{T}
-module
via the action
\star_{T}
defined by

a\star_{T}x\coloneqq T(a)x,\quad\forall x\in H,a\in K.

Proof.

If
T:K\to H
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator, then by Corollary
3.4
,
K_{T}=(K,*_{T})
is an algebra with unit 1. Also,

\displaystyle 1\star_{T}x

\displaystyle=T(1)x=1x=x,

\displaystyle(a*_{T}b)\star_{T}x

\displaystyle=T(a*_{T}b)x=T(a)T(b)x=a\star_{T}(b\star_{T}x),

for any
x\in H,\,a,b\in K
. That is,
H
is a
K_{T}
-module.

Conversely, if
K_{T}=(K,*_{T})
is an algebra and
H
is a
K_{T}
-module via the stated action
\star_{T}
, then particularly

\displaystyle T(a_{1}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b))

\displaystyle=T(a*_{T}b)=T(a*_{T}b)1=(a*_{T}b)\star_{T}1

\displaystyle=a\star_{T}(b\star_{T}1)=T(a)(T(b)1)=T(a)T(b).

Namely, (
17
) holds, and
T:K\to H
is a relative Rota-Baxter operator.
∎

The following result is straightforward to obtain.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
H
and
K
be two cocommutative Hopf algebras such that
K
is an
H
-module bialgebra via an action
\rightharpoonup
. Then
K
is a
K\rtimes H
-module bialgebra defined by

(a\#x).b\coloneqq{\rm ad}_{a}(x\rightharpoonup b),\quad\forall x\in H,\,a,b\in K.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator. Then
K
has a cocommutative
K_{T}
-module bialgebra structure via the following action,

{\rm ad}_{T,a}b\coloneqq{\rm ad}_{a_{1}}(T(a_{2})\rightharpoonup b),\quad\forall a,b\in K.

Proof.

Let
T:K\to H
be a relative Rota-Baxter operator. By Theorem
5.4
, the graph
{\rm Gr}_{T}
is a Hopf algebra inside
K\rtimes H
. Therefore
K
becomes a
{\rm Gr}_{T}
-module bialgebra by Lemma
5.7
.

Furthermore, pulled back by the Hopf algebra isomorphism
\Psi:K_{T}\to{\rm Gr}_{T}
given in (
42
),
K
becomes a
K_{T}
-module bialgebra via the desired action
{\rm ad}_{T}
.
∎
